with an additional axiom requiring that the sort
\mathsf{K}
is inhabited.

.

.

We observe that
\mbox{\clock}\cong\omega^{\times}_{\textit{inh}}
; thus we define the

Sterling–Harper topos
over
\mathscr{S}
to be the algebraic
\mathscr{S}
-topos

{\textbf{{SH}}}\coloneqq\widehat{\omega^{\times}_{\textit{inh}}}
whose total geometric universe is

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}\mkern-1.0mu_{{\textbf{{SH}}}}=\mathbf{Pr}_{\mathscr{S}}{\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\omega^{\times}_{\textit{inh}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\omega^{\times}_{\textit{inh}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\omega^{\times}_{\textit{inh}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\omega^{\times}_{\textit{inh}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}}
.

Lemma 5.11
.

If
E
is the algebraic
\mathscr{S}
-topos presented by an
\mathscr{S}
-category

\mathbb{C}
, then the inhabited bagtopos
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathsf{L}}^{\textit{inh}}{{\textbf{{E}}}}
admits an algebraic
presentation by
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{\textit{inh}}
; in other words, we have

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathsf{L}}^{\textit{inh}}{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}\simeq\widehat{\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{\textit{inh}}}
.

Corollary 5.12

(Universal property)
.

The Sterling–Harper topos
SH
is equivalent to the inhabited bagtopos

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathsf{L}}^{\textit{inh}}{\widehat{\omega}}
, hence
SH
classifies the geometric theory

\mathsf{bag}_{\textit{inh}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{filter}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{filter}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{filter}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{filter}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\omega$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. In other words, the category of morphisms of
topoi
X
SH
is exactly the category of pairs
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle K,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle K,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle K,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle K,\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}

where
K\in\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}\mkern-1.0mu_{{\textbf{{X}}}}
is inhabited and
\phi\subseteq K\times\omega
is a

K
-indexed family of filters on
\omega
internal to
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}\mkern-1.0mu_{{\textbf{{X}}}}
.

6
Lifting guarded recursion to the bagtopos

In this section, we combine the relative point of view with our general
stability results for models of SGDT to obtain an abstract proof that the
Bizjak–Møgelberg topos
BM
carries a model of multi-clock guarded
recursion. The results of this section carry over
mutatis mutandis
to
the other variants of the bagtopos considered in the preceeding section.

Corollary 6.3
.

The Bizjak–Møgelberg topos
BM
carries a globally adequate model of multi-clock synthetic guarded domain theory, parameterized in the generic indexing object
\mathsf{K}\in\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}\mkern-1.0mu_{{\textbf{{BM}}}}
.

Proof.

The result follows from
Lemma

6.2
via
Lemma

4.1
.
Viewing
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{K}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{K}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{K}$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{K}$}\mathclose{}{]}}
as an algebraic topos over
\widehat{\omega}
, we
automatically have a model of synthetic guarded domain theory by

Theorem

3.11
. Global adequacy follows from

Lemma

4.1
via
Lemma

3.10
.
∎

Guarded recursive terms

.

Notation A.1

(Internal language)
.

When working in the internal language of an elementary geometric model

\mathscr{E}
of synthetic guarded domain theory, we will make use of the notations
of guarded dependent type theory
[
12
]
. In particular, we employ
the notation of
delayed substitutions
for the dependent version of the
later modality defined schematically below for a pair of dependent types

\Gamma\vdash A\ \mathit{type}
and
\Gamma,x:A\vdash Bx\ \mathit{type}
:

\Gamma,u:\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{A}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}Bx}
\Gamma\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0,1}\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{A}}
\pi_{\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}Bx}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma,x:A\vdash Bx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma,x:A\vdash Bx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma,x:A\vdash Bx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma,x:A\vdash Bx$}\mathclose{}{)}}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\Gamma\vdash A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Gamma\vdash A$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Gamma\vdash A$}\mathclose{}{)}}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\pi_{Bx}
\Gamma
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{\Gamma}
\Gamma\vdash A
\Gamma
\Gamma,x:A\vdash Bx
\mathsf{next}_{B}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}
\mathsf{next}_{A}

.

See
3.6

Proof.

The only-if direction is immediate. For the converse, we will employ the
principle of unique choice. In particular, we consider the predicate

x:A\mid\phi\,x
defined like so:

\phi{x}\coloneqq\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle x=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\land\forall y.y=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}\Rightarrow y=x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle x=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\land\forall y.y=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}\Rightarrow y=x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle x=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\land\forall y.y=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}\Rightarrow y=x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle x=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,x$}\mathclose{}{)}}\land\forall y.y=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}\Rightarrow y=x$}\mathclose{}{)}}

To show that there exists
x:A
such that
\phi{x}
holds, it suffices by
Löb induction to assume
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\exists x:A.\phi{x}
and then exhibit
x:A

satisfying
\phi{x}
. By
A.2
we may assume that
there exists some
u:\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{A}
such that
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle z\leftarrow{}u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle z\leftarrow{}u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z\leftarrow{}u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z\leftarrow{}u$}\mathclose{}{]}}\phi{z}

holds. We choose
x\coloneqq fu
and must check that
\phi\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle fx$}\mathclose{}{)}}
holds.

(1)

Existence.
To check that
fu=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, we will verify that

u=\mathsf{next}_{A}\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}
in
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{A}
, or equivalently

\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle z=fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle z=fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z=fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z=fu$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. By well-pointedness, this is
the same as
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle z=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle z=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
;
but

z=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,z$}\mathclose{}{)}}
follows from
\phi{z}
, hence our assumption

\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}\phi{z}
suffices.

(2)

Uniqueness.
Next we must check that for all
y:A
where
y=f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, we have
y=fu
; in other words, we must check that

f\,\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y$}\mathclose{}{)}}=fu
. By congruence with
f
it suffices to check
that
\mathsf{next}_{A}\,y=u
in
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{A}
, which (as above) is the same as to
check that
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle y=z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle y=z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle y=z$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle y=z$}\mathclose{}{)}}
. This again follows from
our assumption
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\displaystyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\textstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}{\mathopen{}{[}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle z\leftarrow u$}\mathclose{}{]}}\phi{z}
.
∎

A.2
Stability properties for geometric models of SGDT

Observation A.3
.

If the internal category
\mathbb{C}
has a terminal object, then the
pointwise later modality of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}\mkern-1.0mu_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
commutes with global
sections in the sense that the following canonical 2-cell is an isomorphism:

\beta
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}^{\mathbb{C}}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}\mkern-1.0mu_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
\mathscr{S}

The 2-cell
\beta
depicted above is the distribution of
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}
over the limit of a given presheaf.

See
3.10

Proof.

We want to show that the following 2-cell is an isomorphism:

\mathsf{next}^{\mathbb{C}}
\mathbb{N}
\mathbb{N}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}^{\mathbb{C}}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{S}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{S}
\mathbf{1}
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}\mkern-1.0mu_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
\mathscr{S}
\mathscr{U}

(1)

We may paste an isomorphism onto
Eq.

1
and check that the result
is an isomorphism: the left-hand isomorphism witnesses the preservation of the
natural numbers object by
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
and the right-hand
isomorphism is from
A.3
:

\cong
\mathsf{next}^{\mathbb{C}}
\beta^{-1}
\mathbb{N}
\mathbb{N}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{S}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{S}
\mathbf{1}
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}\mkern-1.0mu_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
\mathscr{S}
\mathscr{U}

(2)

Eq.

2
is equal to the following, which is an isomorphism by assumption:

\cong
\mathsf{next}
\mathbb{N}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
\mathbb{N}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{S}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{S}
\mathbf{1}
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}\mkern-1.0mu_{\widehat{\mathbb{C}}}
\mathscr{S}
\mathscr{U}

(3)

Thus
Eq.

1
is an isomorphism.
∎

See
3.13

Proof.

We proceed by rewiring in several steps.

\mathsf{next}_{L}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}_{L}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}_{L}

(4)

First we unfold definitions.

\epsilon^{-1}
\mathsf{next}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{L}
\mathbf{\Delta}_{L}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{L}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}
\mathbf{\Delta}_{L}

(5)

Next we rewire using the fact that under direct image the inverse to the counit becomes the unit.

\mathsf{next}
\eta
\mathbf{\Delta}_{L}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{L}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{L}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}
\mathbf{\Delta}_{L}

(6)

Then we rewire using our assumption that
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright},\mathsf{next}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright},\mathsf{next}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright},\mathsf{next}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright},\mathsf{next}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
is well-pointed.

\mathsf{next}
\eta
\mathbf{\Delta}_{L}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{L}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{L}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}
\mathbf{\Delta}_{L}

(7)

We rewire using the fact that the unit becomes the inverse to the counit under inverse image.

\mathsf{next}
\epsilon^{-1}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}
\mathop{\blacktriangleright}
\mathbf{\Delta}_{L}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{L}
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{L}
\mathbf{\Delta}_{L}

(8)

Folding definitions, we are done.

A.3
A base geometric model of SGDT

In this section, recall that
\mathbb{A}
is a frame in a geometric universe
\mathscr{S}

equipped with a well-founded basis
\mathbb{K}
. We will write

i:\hbox to28.03pt{\vbox to7.55pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.77779pt\lower-0.33063pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

for the corresponding embedding of

\mathscr{S}
-topoi. We have obtained a well-pointed later modality structure

\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright},\mathsf{next}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright},\mathsf{next}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright},\mathsf{next}$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright},\mathsf{next}$}\mathclose{}{)}}
on
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}\mkern-1.0mu_{\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}}
from the well-pointed later modality
structure
\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\widehat{\mathsf{p}}^{*},\nu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\widehat{\mathsf{p}}^{*},\nu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\widehat{\mathsf{p}}^{*},\nu$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\widehat{\mathsf{p}}^{*},\nu$}\mathclose{}{)}}
on
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}\mkern-1.0mu_{\widehat{\mathbb{A}}}
using
3.12
, but it remains to show
that the former supports Löb induction.

See
3.22

Proof.

This is easily verified in the Kripke-Joyal semantics of
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{S}}\mkern-1.0mu_{\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}}
. Fixing

u\in\mathbb{A}
and a closed sieve
\phi\in\Omega{u}
such that
u\Vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{\phi}\Rightarrow\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{\phi}\Rightarrow\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{\phi}\Rightarrow\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{\phi}\Rightarrow\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\top
we must check that
u\Vdash\phi=\top
.
As
\mathbb{K}
is a basis for
\mathbb{A}
, we know that
u={\mathchoice{\textstyle\bigvee}{\textstyle\bigvee}{\scriptstyle\bigvee}{\scriptscriptstyle\bigvee}}_{k\in\mathbb{K}_{u}}k
, so by local
character it suffices to verify that
k\Vdash k^{*}\phi=\top
for each
k\in\mathbb{K}_{u}
.
By well-founded induction we may assume that
l\Vdash l^{*}\phi=\top
for all

l\prec k
. From this assumption we have
k\Vdash\widehat{\mathsf{p}}^{*}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k^{*}\phi=\top$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k^{*}\phi=\top$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k^{*}\phi=\top$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k^{*}\phi=\top$}\mathclose{}{)}}
and hence
k\Vdash\mathop{\blacktriangleright}\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle k^{*}\phi=\top$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle k^{*}\phi=\top$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle k^{*}\phi=\top$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle k^{*}\phi=\top$}\mathclose{}{)}}
, so by our assumption
that
u\Vdash\mathchoice{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{\phi}\Rightarrow\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\textstyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{\phi}\Rightarrow\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{\phi}\Rightarrow\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}{\mathopen{}{(}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathop{\blacktriangleright}{\phi}\Rightarrow\phi$}\mathclose{}{)}}=\top
we are done.
∎
